sclet: A Lightweight Toolkit for Single-Cell Data Analysis

7 Integration and Batch Correction

This mainline answers the multi-sample question: how do we move several batches, donors, or studies into one comparable analysis space without losing track of the corrected view?

In sclet, integration is not treated as an isolated algorithm demo. It is a reusable infrastructure step that downstream mainlines can inherit. This chapter therefore focuses on how RunIntegration() records corrected layers or reductions and how those results remain available to later analysis steps.

7.1 Load datasets

We load the PBMC 3k and 4k datasets using TENxPBMCData. Note that these datasets use Ensembl IDs as row names by default. To facilitate downstream analysis (e.g., cell type annotation with SingleR which requires Gene Symbols matching the reference), we map the Ensembl IDs to Gene Symbols. We use scuttle::uniquifyFeatureNames() to perform this mapping while ensuring that the resulting row names remain unique.

library(TENxPBMCData)
library(scuttle)

pbmc3k <- TENxPBMCData(dataset = "pbmc3k")
# Replace Ensembl IDs with Gene Symbols (ensuring uniqueness) for compatibility with downstream tools
rownames(pbmc3k) <- scuttle::uniquifyFeatureNames(rownames(pbmc3k), rowData(pbmc3k)$Symbol_TENx)

pbmc4k <- TENxPBMCData(dataset = "pbmc4k")
rownames(pbmc4k) <- scuttle::uniquifyFeatureNames(rownames(pbmc4k), rowData(pbmc4k)$Symbol_TENx)

7.2 Preprocess

library(sclet)

# Apply standard QC thresholds before normalization
sce_process <- function(sce) {
  sce <- QCMetrics(sce)
  sce[["percent.mt"]] <- PercentageFeatureSet(sce, "^MT-")
  keep <- colData(sce)$nFeature_RNA > 200 &
    colData(sce)$nFeature_RNA < 2500 &
    colData(sce)$percent.mt < 5
  sce <- sce[, keep, drop = FALSE]
  sce <- NormalizeData(sce)
  sce <- FindVariableFeatures(sce)
  return(sce)
}

pbmc3k <- sce_process(pbmc3k)
pbmc4k <- sce_process(pbmc4k)

pbmc_list <- list(pbmc3k = pbmc3k, pbmc4k = pbmc4k)

7.3 Integration Workflow

sclet provides a unified RunIntegration entry point to perform multi-backend data integration (e.g., fastMNN, Harmony). It automatically manages the resulting representations (layers or reductions) and registers an active integration state so that downstream tools can consume them seamlessly.

# Merge datasets
combined_pbmc <- sce_merge(pbmc_list)

# Integration using fastMNN (default)
rm_batch_pbmc <- RunIntegration(combined_pbmc, method = "fastMNN", batch = "batch")

# RunIntegration registers the logical "corrected" layer for fastMNN
DefaultLayer(rm_batch_pbmc)
## [1] "corrected"
Layers(rm_batch_pbmc)
## [1] "corrected" "counts"    "logcounts"
# and records the typed integration state
has_integration(rm_batch_pbmc)
## [1] TRUE
get_integration(rm_batch_pbmc)
## $id

Alternatively, you can use Harmony or scVI (Deep Learning via basilisk), which operate on the latent space and return a corrected reduction rather than an expression layer:

The Harmony example is executed during book rendering, which means it also serves as a regression test in CI. The scVI call is now cached through bookdown/data/rm_scvi_pbmc.rds, so the book no longer provisions the Python-backed basilisk environment during render.

# Integration using Harmony
rm_harmony_pbmc <- RunIntegration(combined_pbmc, method = "Harmony", batch = "batch")

# Harmony creates a reduction named "HARMONY" and sets it as the active reduction
DefaultReduction(rm_harmony_pbmc)
rm_scvi_pbmc <- readRDS("data/rm_scvi_pbmc.rds")
DefaultReduction(rm_scvi_pbmc)
## [1] "scVI"

Recent sclet versions also tightened the memory behavior of this path. Instead of forcing the count matrix through as.matrix() and materializing a dense block before crossing into Python, the scVI backend now keeps sparse inputs sparse and converts them directly to scipy.sparse.csr_matrix via reticulate. For toy PBMC data this is just cleaner; for large atlas-scale data it is the difference between “runs” and “explodes”.

get_integration(rm_scvi_pbmc)

7.4 Clustering

sce_clustering <- function(sce, layer = DefaultLayer(sce), dims = 1:10) {
  # For uncorrected data, we run PCA first
  if (!"PCA" %in% SingleCellExperiment::reducedDimNames(sce)) {
      sce <- RunPCA(sce, subset_row = VariableFeatures(sce), layer = layer)
  }
  
  # Downstream tools automatically perceive the integration state
  # (e.g., FindNeighbors will use "HARMONY" if it is the active reduction)
  sce <- FindNeighbors(sce, dims = dims)
  sce <- FindClusters(sce)
  sce <- RunUMAP(sce, dims = dims, layer = layer)
  return(sce)
}

combined_pbmc <- sce_clustering(combined_pbmc, layer = "logcounts")

rm_batch_pbmc <- sce_clustering(rm_batch_pbmc, layer = "corrected")

The important point is that downstream analysis functions can now consume the integration outputs directly, without requiring users to manually point to the reconstructed assay or the HARMONY reduction. RunIntegration() sets up the active pointers in the analysis-state contract.

The merge step is also part of that story now. sce_merge() stores a non-active merged_inputs integration record, and BatchRemover() points back to it. This gives the merged object and the corrected object a connected provenance chain instead of two unrelated transformations.

You can inspect how this provenance is carried into downstream states:

7.5 Visualization

library(ggplot2)
library(aplot)

p1 <- DimPlot(combined_pbmc, reduction="UMAP", mapping=aes(color=batch)) 
p2 <- DimPlot(rm_batch_pbmc, reduction="UMAP", mapping=aes(color=batch)) 
p3 <- plot_list(Before=p1, After=p2)

p4 <- DimPlot(rm_batch_pbmc, reduction="UMAP")

plot_list(p3, p4, ncol=1, heights=c(1, 2))

7.6 Pseudobulk Differential Expression

sclet now supports pseudobulk analysis to perform robust differential expression between conditions/samples.

This pseudobulk example is also shown without execution, because real use requires meaningful biological replicates and an analysis-specific design formula. In other words, the missing piece here is experimental design rather than software support: a documentation build can fabricate toy labels, but it cannot invent biologically valid replicate structure.

# 1. Aggregate counts by cluster and batch (or sample)
# This creates a pseudobulk SummarizedExperiment
pb <- AggregateExpression(rm_batch_pbmc, group_by = c("label", "batch"))

# 2. Perform DE analysis (using DESeq2)
# Here we test for differences between batches within a specific cluster (e.g., cluster 1)
# Note: Real analysis should have biological replicates, here we use batch as an example.

# Filter for cluster 1 only
pb_cluster1 <- pb[, pb$label == "1"]

# Run DE: test for batch effect
# design formula: ~ batch
# contrast: batch pbmc4k vs pbmc3k
